## D178. Reverb length measured; parts A/B parked (2026-11-15)

With the user. The open items around effects were taken up (macros reaching the effects, saving
one's own sounds, a reverb-length test); asked whether the two parts of a sound (A and B, D70-D75)
are still a good idea, the user **parked** that question to talk it through later. Options laid
out: two sounds stacked from the browser (Hydrasynth Multi, Montage, Kronos Combinations; the
recommendation), keep as it is, drop part B, talk it through first (chosen). Only Supersaw, Juno
Pad and Deep Field play a part B. What follows is built for the two parts as they are.

- **The reverb-length test** (tests/V3ReverbTests.cpp): RT60 as T30, the Schroeder backward
  integral from -5 to -35 dB, doubled (ISO 3382). The plate at damping 0.3 rings 2.41, 4.36 and
  10.49 s at sizes 0.2, 0.5 and 0.8; the room at damping 0.5 rings 0.15, 0.32 and 0.66 s. The
  test holds each within a fifth of that and longer with size. Options for the band: **a fifth
  (chosen)**, tight enough to show a change to the tank, loose enough for a retuned allpass; a
  tenth (would fail on harmless changes); only "longer with size" (a halved tail would pass).
  Worth knowing: the plate's smallest size is still a 2.4 s space; short rooms are the Room
  effect's.

## D179. Effect modulation: the part's global sources move the effects (2026-11-15)

The user's choice: "full effect modulation + effect routes" (options: effect routes from the macros
only, recommended; leave the macros voice-only, as knob 8 already moves every reverb and delay;
full effect modulation; the user took the full one, which holds the macro routes). Sources: Serum
and Vital macros reach effect mix and size (MusicRadar, "How to make macro assignments that
matter"; Vital forum), Kontakt instruments give a macro up to 12 assignments.
- **Routes**: eight per part (pA.fx1 .. pA.fx8: source, destination, amount, via, curve, as the
  matrix). Destinations: every setting of the part's inserts I1-I3 and of the master F1-F6 that
  the slot's type lets move (the descriptor's modulatable flag; a choice such as a delay's sync is
  left alone). Added to the setting's normalised value and held in 0..1, as the voice matrix does.
  Both parts' routes may move the master slots.
- **Sources**: only what is the same for every voice of a part: the four macros, both global LFOs,
  the global envelope, the step modulator, chaos, the mod wheel, pitch bend, key pressure and the
  expression pedal. A voice's envelope or LFO cannot drive an effect all voices share; the bank
  refuses one with a sentence saying so.
- **Smooth**: the sources are read at a block's start and end and the effects run in steps of 32
  samples between them; with the macros' own 10 ms glide and the effects' own smoothing a macro
  turned at once does not step. A block where no route adds anything runs the effects in one piece,
  as before: every preset renders bit for bit as it did. Options for the step: **32 samples
  (chosen)**, the voices' own sub-block; 64 (half the calls, coarser for a fast LFO); per sample
  (an effect is not built to change every sample, and costs most).
- **Bank**: "fx = src=macro2 dst=f1.mix amount=0.3", "b.fx = ..." for part B; a destination by
  the effect's own key in this preset ("i1.feedback", "f2.size") or by number ("f2.p3").
- Tested: the bank lines (and both refusals); a route adding nothing leaves the output bit for bit;
  a macro on the master reverb's mix makes the tail 7.8 dB louder; turning it at once does not step.
  The parameters grow by 80 (eight routes of five, per part); older states load with every route Off.
Next: the presets' macros get effect routes where an effect fits their role, drafted as in D177.

## D180. My Sounds: saving one's own sounds (2026-11-15)

The user's choices: **a My Sounds room** (options: a My Sounds room in Home, recommended; file
save and open only; only the host saves), kept in **Documents\CoolSynth\My Sounds** (options:
Documents, recommended; hidden app data; next to the sample library). Pigments and Vital save a
copy of a factory sound into a user bank the same way (Arturia and Vital forums).
- **Save**: Options, "Save sound...", asks for a name (the sound's own offered); a name already in
  My Sounds is replaced only after asking. A name loses what Windows forbids in a file name. One
  .cspatch file per sound (the existing format): every parameter, one's own sample files, and the
  sound's macro names and engine pills, so the room shows them without reading the whole sound.
- **Browse**: "My Sounds" is the first room in Home as soon as it holds a sound, A to Z, with pills
  and the playing mark, on the panel and the MiniLab alike; the folder is read again each time Home
  opens, so a file copied in shows up.
- **Load**: as a host's saved state: the sound arrives whole (D157), notes ringing keep the sound
  before (D158), its macro names on the Macros page.
- Later: renaming and deleting from the panel (today: in the folder), and saving from the MiniLab.
Tested in a temporary folder: a clean name, save, the refusal and the replace, loading back (the
macro as saved, the names, the pills), the room first in Home, loading from it, the playing mark.

## D181. The presets' macros move their effects; LoFi's wow glides (2026-11-15)

With D179 built, four drafting agents (Opus, high effort; brief docs/macro-drafts/BRIEF-fx.md)
gave the macros effect routes where an effect makes the macro's role better: 101 routes on 76
presets (drafts with a line per route on what it does: docs/macro-drafts/fx-*.md). Mostly Time
growing a reverb's size or a delay's feedback, Movement deepening a chorus or adding shimmer, a few
Character routes to a drive or tape wear. No macro name changed; the Conservatory's Tone stays
unused (only a reverb there, no EQ). No wet mix is raised; feedback stays at most 0.52.
- Checked as in D177: every line parses; at rest every preset renders bit for bit as before; all
  macros full up stays at most +3.7 dB (Riser, unchanged); turning all four at once does not click.
- **Found and fixed**: Bedroom Bass clicked (6.65x) when its Wobble moved the LoFi's wow. Wow and
  flutter move a delay line's read point, and their depth was taken once per block, so every change
  jumped it (a knob turned by hand did it too, at block edges). The depths now glide over about
  50 ms, starting at their setting after a reset, so a sound at rest is unchanged. Options: **glide
  the depths (chosen)**; interpolate the depth across each block (a corner at every block edge);
  keep wow out of the routes (the fault stays for the knob).
- For the user's ear: the layer presets (Deep Field, Juno Pad) route the same master slot from both
  parts, so their amounts add when both parts' knobs are up; the shimmer on four Conservatory bells;
  +0.15 reverb size on strings and organs at full Linger.

## D182. Browsing with a mouse and a computer keyboard, without a MiniLab (2026-11-15)

The user: "make the software UI easier to browse... don't change the visuals... if I click the back
button, it should go back; if I click the upper menu labels it should work... from the point of
view of a user that doesn't have a MiniLab 3"; "make sure the mouse wheel works at least for
scrolling". Nothing drawn changed.
- Found: in a long room (the Conservatory, 40 sounds) the list scrolls with the cursor and its
  ‹ Back row leaves the screen, and the wheel did nothing on the screen: a mouse could not get back.
- **The wheel** scrolls the list it is over (rooms, sounds, type lists, a section's slots), turns a
  card's knob over a card, and the pages over the page tabs. Tested with a real wheel event on the
  drawn screen.
- **Back** from anywhere: the chip in the header, a right click, Escape or Backspace, besides the
  ‹ Back row: a type list closes, a sound list goes to the rooms, a section goes Home.
- **The trail** is clickable: Categories and the room go there, the sound's name goes Home, the ◎
  item presses the main knob, the page item turns to the next page (round again after the last).
- **"Part A"** in the header switches the part (Shift + pad 8). A slot clicked again opens its type
  list. The pointer becomes a hand over all of these, as it already did over rows and cards.
- **Keys**: up/down now move through a list where one is shown (they turned knob 5's pages, which
  surprised in a vertical list; in a section's map they still do); Page Up/Down five rows; Home/End
  the ends. Options for up/down: **move the list where there is one (chosen)**; keep them on knob 5
  everywhere (as documented before); swap left/right and up/down everywhere (breaks the habit of
  left/right as the main knob).

## D183. A turned knob shows its value in its middle, then fades (2026-11-15)

The user: "when you're turning the knobs, show in a proper way in the middle of the knob the value,
and when you stop turning, fade it out." On the software panel, knobs 1-8: the value as the model
gives it ("3200 Hz", "+8 %"; knob 5 its page), on a dark disc over the knob's middle so the pointer
does not cross the text, at most two lines, narrowed to fit. Shown while the knob turns and 0.7 s
after the last turn, then faded out over 0.4 s; each turn brings it back. It follows the model's
turn pop-up, so a turn by the mouse, the wheel or the MiniLab's own knobs all show. Options for the
timing: **0.7 s hold, 0.4 s fade (chosen)**, long enough to read a value after letting go; no hold
(gone before it is read); 2 s (lingers over the next turn of another knob). Drawn: build/panel-knobvalue.png.

## D184. Knob values on the knob's grey; pads reordered; Hold, octave and faders without a MiniLab (2026-11-15)

The user, after trying the panel and the MiniLab. Asked (pick lists): the pink pad, Hold, the faders.
- **Knob values** (D183): the disc is the knob's own grey, not the screen's black. A number gets a
  smaller disc with the same text, so more of the pointer shows as it turns; a name keeps the larger.
- **Pads, left to right**: Home, Sound, Shape, Motion, Stop, Play, Record, Effects (was Sound,
  Shape, Motion, Effects, the recorder, Home). Shift + Home switches the part. The MiniLab's pads
  follow, as do the computer keys 1-8.
- **MiniLab pad colours**, by the user's eye: only Record was right. The LEDs get their own colours
  (miniLabColour), the screen keeps its own: Home what Play was sent before (it lit teal), Play a
  plain green, Stop a warmer white (white lit blue), Sound a redder orange (orange lit light pink;
  the user's choice: "Sound: make it orange"); Shape, Motion, Effects and Record as they were. To be
  confirmed on the device.
- **Hold and Oct -/+** work on screen when no MiniLab is connected and grey out when one is (it has
  its own). Hold, the user's choice "like the MiniLab": with the arp off it holds the notes like a
  sustain pedal, with the arp on it latches the arpeggio (as the MiniLab 3 does: Arturia forum,
  "Why doesn't the hold button hold notes when the arp is off"). Oct -/+ shift the screen's keys up
  to three octaves. Options for Hold: **like the MiniLab (chosen)**; always sustain; always arp latch.
- **The faders**, the user's choice "volume on top of the sound": four new volumes (volumeA,
  volumeB, volumeFx, volumeMaster), at the top the sound as designed, a cube taper (half way about
  -18 dB, the bottom off), all four starting at the top and kept when the sound changes (a preset
  or a saved sound never moves them). Before, the faders wrote the sound's own levels: a part's
  fader at the top was +6 dB and its designed level sat at 90 %, and the master fader overwrote the
  level each preset was balanced at (D153), so the MiniLab's positions meant nothing fixed. The
  MiniLab's faders are now taken as they are (no catching up); while it is connected the screen's
  faders follow them and do not take the mouse. Options: **volumes over the sound (chosen)**; keep
  writing the sound's levels, rescaled. At the top every preset renders bit for bit as before.

- **Colours settled on the device and the screen** (same day): the MiniLab's pads by the user's eye
  ("the colors on the pad are perfect"): Home a bright teal (#4aff8e sent), Shape a clear blue
  (#1060ff sent), Stop a white with full green (#d7ffbe sent), Play green, Sound orange. On the
  screen Shape is now a clear, not dark, blue #3b8bff (was cyan #35b8e0), at the user's word.

functions (pad 2 the pad bank, pad 3 the program, which would take it out of the DAW program
CoolSynth uses) and 4-7 in transport colours; our colours sent again under Shift do not show.

## D190. Layer modes: Stack, Split, Velocity (2026-11-15)

The user's choice after talking it through (options: **Stack, Split, Velocity, recommended and chosen**;
Stack and Split only; keep A, Stack, Split, B), checked online: Sequential's Prophet Rev2 offers Stack
and Split, Roland's Jupiter-X adds a velocity switch, Korg's wavestate crossfades layers by velocity.
The defaults should suit a beginner (the user), so:

- **The modes** are Stack (both layers on every key), Split (each layer inside its key range) and
  Velocity (soft notes to layer A, hard ones to B). The old A-only and B-only are gone: a layer with
  every generator and processor slot Off is **empty and left out**, so one layer alone is a Stack with
  the other empty. Layer B starts empty; Stack is the default.
- **Velocity** has a split velocity (part.velSplit, 2-127, default 64) and a fade (part.velFade, 0-64
  velocities, default 16) over which A gives way to B with an equal-power crossfade, so a note in the
  middle is as loud as one outside it. A note's share rides with it through chord memory, the arp
  (the share of the last key given to it) and a mono part's held keys.
- **Old states and saved sounds** are carried over: A becomes Stack with layer B emptied, Layer
  becomes Stack, Split stays Split, B becomes Stack with layer A emptied (state version 11, patch
  version 10; the versions before are read and converted). Bank files say "parts = stack | split |
  velocity [split=] [fade=]"; "a" and "layer" still read as stack, the old "b" is an error that says
  what to write instead.
- Checked: every factory preset renders bit for bit the same as before (only the four random-arp
  presets differ, as on every run); the parts, insert, migration, patch and interface tests pass.
- Not yet: choosing the mode, the split point and the velocity split on the panel.

## D192. Five octaves on the screen; an empty layer's Home says so (2026-11-15)

- **The screen's keys**: 25 keys across the whole panel made each white key as wide as it was long.
  Checked online: a real white key is about 6.4 times longer than wide (23.5 x 150 mm; Wikipedia,
  "Musical keyboard"), drawing libraries default to about 4:1 (xpiano), many DAWs simply stretch
  (Cantabile forum), Reason lets the window's width decide how many octaves show. Options: **five
  octaves with the MiniLab's keys marked (chosen, the user's)**; 25 keys at real proportions, small and
  centred; 25 keys in a taller window. The keys are C2 to C7; without a MiniLab the screen's Oct
  buttons move them. The MiniLab's Oct buttons send nothing (tested on the device), so the line over
  its keys follows the notes it plays (the user's choice; options: follow the notes, no line, a fixed
  line): a key outside the line moves it by whole octaves, as little as holds that key.
- **Home on an empty layer** showed fallback macro names (Tone, Character...) for macros that do
  nothing. Asked, the user chose **per layer, empty shown empty** (options: one set of macros for the
  whole sound, recommended; per layer with the empty state; as it was): Home says "Layer B is empty"
  and knobs 1-4 show a dash.
- Then the user's UX review question (the list says "Init Saw playing" while the panel says "Layer B
  is empty"; the screen stays teal while the pad is yellow). Checked online: Korg's wavestate keeps a
  Performance over its Layers (Sound On Sound's review), Arturia users report that Analog Lab's Multi
  macros act on both parts by default and a Part button picks one to edit, and Nielsen Norman Group
  warns that a badly signalled mode causes errors. Options: Home is the sound and layers live only in
  the sections (recommended); keep the switch but make the mode loud; **layer B holds a second sound,
  as Analog Lab's Multi (chosen, the user's)**. That is the next piece of work.

## D193. Basic sounds; CoolSynth starts on Contact; the wheel steps one row; the knobs turn along (2026-11-15)

- **Init became Basic** (the user's choice; options: **Basic, recommended and chosen**; Base, which reads
  as Bass; Plain): Basic Saw, Pulse, Sine, Arp, Noise, FM, Wavetable. "Init" is synth jargon for a
  blank patch.
- **The start sound**: a bare saw is dull, said the user. Offered Cassette Keys (recommended), Juno
  Pad, Grid Keys, Neon Brass; the user chose **Contact Opening II Grit** and renamed it **Contact**. A
  new CoolSynth, in the app or a host, starts on it; tests and tools (no plugin wrapper) keep the
  parameters' own defaults. A host's project and the app's last state load after it, so the app
  reopens on the sound it had.
- **Found on the way, a bug older than today**: a saved state kept the sound's name but not its
  program number, and the browser's "playing" mark and the macro names come from the number. After
  every restart the screen named program 0 (then Init Saw, with its macro names) whatever played.
  The state now keeps the My Sounds file too, and a restore finds the sound again by its file or
  its name (old names read as renamed); a sound the bank lacks is shown as no factory sound, not
  the wrong one.
- **The mouse wheel**: JUCE gives a Windows notch as 0.234 (120 / 2 / 256); times the old 8.5 that
  made 1.99, so a notch moved one row, then two. One notch is now one row (part-notches of a
  high-resolution wheel add up; a trackpad scrolls as before), in the screen and on the knobs.
- **The black knob turns along**: the model counts every turn of the main knob, from the screen's
  wheel, the arrow keys, the mouse or the MiniLab's own knob, and the screen's knob follows that
  count; knob 5 follows the page turns the same way.
- Checked: the wheel moved three rows for three notches and the knob turned with them, in the app.

## D194. Each layer holds a whole sound: CoolSynth becomes bi-timbral (2026-11-15, under way)

The user's UX review of Home on layer B (D192) led to the question what A and B are. Talked through
and checked online: a sound filled in its layer and the global parts (the effects chain at the end,
the arp), so two stacked sounds collided and A and B differed. Korg's wavestate gives each layer a
whole program with its own effects and arp, global only reverb and EQ; Yamaha's Montage has insert
effects per part and shared system effects; Roland's Fantom effects per part and shared chorus and
reverb. Such a synth is bi-timbral (Sequential's Prophet Rev2: Layers A and B, each with its own
effect and arp, stacked or split). The user's choices:

- **A layer is a whole sound** (options: layer = whole sound, recommended and chosen; shared reverb
  and delay sends; keep as is): each layer gets six effect slots (was three) and its own arp; A and
  B are alike and either may be empty. Global keeps the layer mode, a chain at the end (empty by
  default, for the combination), the master level, Brightness and Space, the faders and the tempo.
  Measured: no preset uses more than four effect slots in all, so every sound fits its layer; 118
  presets use one layer and move their effects into it (Frozen Pluck alone sets a level of its own,
  before a reverb, which does not mind the order); the 3 that use both (Deep Field, Juno Pad,
  Supersaw) keep their shared effects in the chain at the end. Memory: about 2.5 MB more per slot
  (a grain buffer and a delay line), some 60 MB in all.
- **Home picks a sound for the layer you are on**; picking on A replaces layer A only (options:
  replace A only, recommended and chosen; replace the whole sound). Each list starts with "Empty
  layer A/B" (options: an Empty row, recommended and chosen; a long press on Home).
  **Combinations load whole** (options: whole, recommended and chosen; only their layer A), marked
  "A+B" in the list.
- Stages: the engine (six slots, an arp per layer, old states carried over, every preset bit for
  bit the same); the presets' effects into their layers (checked by rendering each before and
  after); Home and the Effects section.
- **Stage 1 done**: six slots per layer (I1-I6; effect routes aimed at a master slot moved past the
  new ones), an arp per layer ("pA.arpEnabled", "pB.arpEnabled", ...; the follow flag mix.arp gone;
  the tempo stays the sound's). State version 12, patch version 11; an older one is converted (each
  layer's arp on only if it was on and that layer followed it; learned MIDI follows) and whatever it
  lacks comes in at its default. That last step also mends a gap of D190: the split and fade it
  added were missing from older states and saved sounds, which the loader then refused. Found on
  the way: the engine judged "the same sound loaded again" by comparing its settings byte for byte,
  padding included, so a change of stack layout made a preset loaded twice count as new; a load now
  counts as a new sound only if a parameter value changed. All 468 tests pass; every factory preset
  renders bit for bit the same (the four random-arp presets differ, as on every run).
- **Stage 2 done**: the 117 one-layer presets carry their sound in layer A: their master effects
  became layer slots after their own inserts (in order), their effect routes followed, and their
  master level joined the layer level (the master at 0 dB); the 3 combinations keep their shared
  effects in the chain at the end. Checked by rendering every preset from the bank before and after
  with the same program (a chord, 3 s, 24-bit): 115 identical bit for bit, Frozen Pluck within one
  24-bit step (its level before the reverb now comes after it: rounding, -127 dB), the four
  random-arp presets random as ever. Saved states and sounds from before keep their effects in the
  chain at the end, which plays them exactly as before.
- **Stage 3 (Home) done**: Home picks a sound for the layer it is on. A single sound fills that layer
  and leaves the other layer, the chain at the end and the master as they are; beside an empty layer
  a pick is a whole new sound, so its global settings come too (the master's default is -6 dB, the
  sounds now set 0). A combination loads whole, from either layer, and is marked "A+B" in the list.
  Derived rule: a single sound picked for one layer of a combination playing takes the combination
  apart (the other layer empties, the global settings become the sound's), since the pair shared its
  effects. The categories list starts with "Empty layer A/B". Each layer keeps its own sound's name
  and macro names; the state keeps both. Home takes the layer's colour: teal for A, yellow for B
  (the chip, the selected row, the Macros page), answering the user's review that the screen stayed
  teal while the pad was yellow.

## D195. The layer screen above knobs 1-4; rings on knobs 6-8 (2026-11-15)

- **The layer screen**, the user's request and choice from three designs (two lit panels with an
  info row, recommended and chosen; one wide second screen; small panels without a new strip),
  placed where the user said: above knobs 1-4, as wide as from knob 1 to knob 4; the top row of
  knobs moved down under it, the bottom row a little. Two panels, LAYER A teal and LAYER B yellow,
  each with what it plays (bold) or "empty"; the layer the sections edit is lit, the other dim; a
  click on a panel picks its layer. Under them the layer mode, the tempo followed (the host's while
  it plays) and the voices sounding; the CPU sits in its bottom right corner (moved from beside the
  main knob, D191). When the chain at the end gets its own page (D194), both panels go dark and
  a MASTER mark lights.
- **Knobs 6-8 had no ring** and looked inactive (the user). Brightness and Space act on the whole
  sound: a light grey ring, as the user asked. Glide belongs to the edited layer: a ring in that
  layer's colour, teal or yellow, as the layer screen.

## D196. Layer actions and a Layers page (2026-11-15)

The user asked what people do with two layers. Checked online: Sequential's Prophet Rev2 has a copy
and swap page for its layers and, since OS 1.0.7.2, puts a sound from another program on a layer;
Cherry Audio's Memorymode has a layer utility (swap, copy, paste, reset), a Balance knob and Solo
Layer; layering tips (MusicRadar, Harmony Central, Craig Anderton, Sound On Sound) double a sound
and detune the copies (about 5 cents for strings, 40-60 for leads), add an octave layer below for
weight or above for sparkle, stack an attack on a pad, and switch or crossfade by velocity; Korg's
multitimbral synths give each part a detune of +-50 cents.

- **Actions, all four chosen**: copy A to B or B to A, swap, empty a layer; Double (copy the edited
  layer to the other and pull them apart a few cents and a little left and right); Octave layer
  (the copy an octave down or up, a little quieter); Solo layer while editing.
- **Where**: a **Layers page in Home** (chosen, recommended; options: the Layers page, a right-click
  menu on the layer screen, both): knob 5's sixth live page, knobs 1-4 Balance A/B, Detune spread,
  the other layer's octave and Stereo spread; the actions in the main knob's list on that page, so
  they work from the MiniLab too.
- **Built**: three parameters between the layers, part.balance (-1..1; toward one layer the other
  goes quieter along a quarter cosine, off at the end), part.detune (0-50 cents, half each way) and
  part.spread (A to the left, B to the right); at 0 they leave the mix untouched, so every preset
  sounds as before. Knob 3 is the other layer's Transpose (semitones, so an octave is 12 detents).
  The actions, for the layer Home is on (X) and the other (Y): Copy X to Y, Copy Y to X, Swap A and B,
  Double X onto Y (14 cents apart, half spread, each 3 dB down so the pair is as loud as one), X an
  octave lower or higher on Y (6 dB under), Solo X (not saved; the layer screen says SOLO and MUTED),
  Empty layer Y. Each is one undo step. A copy is no combination: loading a sound into one of two
  copied layers keeps the other (a bug the tests caught: copies share a program, and were taken for a
  combination to take apart). State version 13, patch version 12; older ones get the new settings at
  their defaults.
- **The Effects section and MASTER** (same day): the section's map shows the layer's six slots (I1-I6, two
  rows, LAYER) and the chain at the end (F1-F6, two rows, MASTER); before, it still drew three layer slots
  and cut F4-F6 off. With the cursor on an F slot both panels of the layer screen go dark and MASTER lights in
  its info row (D195).

## D197. Prefs as a dialog (2026-11-15)

At the user's word ("the options menu should be a dialog box, properly designed"), Prefs is a card
in the panel's own style over the dimmed panel, where the menu of D174 was: SOUND (Save sound...),
AUDIO AND MIDI (in the app), MULTI-CORE (Auto or Off as a row of buttons, a line saying what the
choice does), VELOCITY CURVE (Soft, Normal, Hard, likewise) and THIS COMPUTER, THE LAST SECOND (the
CPU's work in detail, the machine and its helpers, live). The x, Escape or a click beside the card
closes it; the Prefs button and the CPU reading open it. The card lays itself out each time it opens,
since the app hands the panel its audio settings only after the panel is built.

## D198. Never "Init" (2026-11-15)

The user asked where "Init" came from and not to start in it. "Init" is the name CoolSynth gave its
built-in default settings before D193, when a new instance played no sound from the list; the app
saved that state and, reopening on it, saved it again, so it lived on until a sound was picked. Now a
state that is that untouched default ("Init", not edited) gives way to the start sound, Contact, when
the app or a host opens it; an edited one stays. (Found the same hour, at the user's question about
notes repeating like an arp: the flight log showed them arriving that way, the same velocity every
0.1-0.2 s, before CoolSynth touched them; the user found the MiniLab's own arpeggiator on.)

## D199. Preferences: a Reset, the title, Audio and MIDI (2026-11-15)

At the user's word: the dialog's title is "Preferences"; Audio and MIDI settings open in their own
window with Preferences still open behind them (it closed before, and was gone when the user came
back); and a **Reset** section. Asked what it resets (options: everything but My Sounds,
recommended; the sound only; Preferences only), the user chose **the sound only**: Contact on layer
A, layer B empty, the faders at the top, the Layers page and knobs 7-8 centred, solo off; the
Preferences choices and MIDI learn stay. It asks first, inside the card (Reset / Cancel), and takes the
panel back to Home on layer A.

## D200. MiniLab knobs reach both ends; solo plays a layer as it is (2026-11-15)

Two faults the user found:

- **A MiniLab knob stopped short** of a setting's end (knob 2, Detune). The knobs turn endlessly, like
  a scroll wheel (the user: "they keep on turning forever"), but in the DAW program each sends a count,
  0-127, that stops at its ends; CoolSynth took each change as clicks and ignored a count that did not
  change, so a knob whose count sat near an end ran out. A first fix scaled what was left of the count's
  travel over what was left of the setting's range (Ableton Live's takeover "Value Scaling", Live manual
  "MIDI and Key Remote Control"; an Arturia forum post on the MiniLab 3's absolute knobs in DAW mode);
  it worked (the user confirmed Detune 0.0 to 50.0 ct) but left a knob whose count sits at an end on a
  new page stuck. A guided test on the device (the count set from the computer, three small turns)
  showed two things: the MiniLab ignores a count sent back to it (it went on from 58 after 0 was sent),
  and turning on past an end sends that end again (127, 127, 127). So, as the user put it, the knob is a
  scroll wheel: every change of the count is that many clicks, and the end sent again is one more click;
  the Value Scaling is gone. Then the user found switches and short lists far too quick (on/off, the
  chord): about 40 counts make a quarter turn, so from the MiniLab a switch now moves a step per 10
  counts, a list (and knob 5's pages) an option per 6, a number a step per count; turning the other way,
  or another setting, starts afresh. The mouse wheel on screen keeps one notch per option. Confirmed on
  the device by the user ("good now").
- **Solo A played in the left speaker only**: Spread (set by Double) pushes A left and B right, and
  stayed on with A alone. Balance, Detune and Spread now act only while both layers sound: a soloed
  layer, or one beside an empty layer, plays as it is. Confirmed by the user (both speakers).

## D201. A knob that does nothing now shows dimmed (2026-11-15)

The user, on Spread having no effect in solo: shouldn't it be greyed out, "maybe on others as
well?" Asked (options: dimmed and still turnable, recommended and chosen; disabled; and where:
everywhere it happens, recommended and chosen; the Layers page only): a setting that does nothing at
the moment shows a faint ring, a grey name and value, and its screen card says when it acts; it still
turns, so it can be set ahead. Where: Balance, Detune and Spread while a layer is soloed or the other
layer is empty ("SOLO", "BOTH LAYERS"); the other layer's Transpose when that layer is empty ("B
EMPTY"); the Arp page's Rate, Pattern and Octaves and the Groove page while the layer's arp is off
("ARP OFF"); Glide on an empty layer ("LAYER EMPTY").

## D202. Knob 5 in white (2026-11-15)

"Live Page is a very important knob" (the user). Its faint blue ring became bright white, unlike
every section's colour (options: bright white, recommended and chosen; a strong blue, close to
Shape's; the page's own colour, which would look like knobs 1-4); the screen's current page tab is

## D203. "Your keyboard" over the screen's keys (2026-11-15)

The user did not know what "MiniLab keys" over the screen's keyboard was, had it taken away, then asked it back
in the line's colour, since it moves with the line, and named for what it is, not the device. It reads

## D204. What can be clicked or turned lights up under the mouse (2026-11-15)

"If you have a clickable or scrollable thing, it should glow up" when the mouse is over it (the
user). Asked, after looking at how others do it (options: glow and a hint line, recommended and
chosen; glow only; the hand cursor only, as before): a knob under the mouse gets a soft halo in its
ring colour, a pad a soft glow, a fader thumb turns white, the Prefs, Hold and Oct buttons brighten;
on the screen a list row, tab, slot or card gets a soft outline, and so do the two layer panels and
the CPU reading. The hand cursor shows over all of them, and the screen's hint line says what the
thing is and how to work it ("Knob 2: Grit · turn, drag or scroll", "Minimoog Fat Bass · click"),
falling back to the usual hint when the mouse leaves; the hint line now always keeps its row. Asked
too (edge fade, recommended and chosen; leave the lists as they are): when more rows wait above or
below, that edge of a list fades out over 22 px; the fade goes at the end of the list. Sources: a
status line naming the hovered control in DecentSampler (decentsamples.com Q&A 414); clickability
cues (design-to-dev.netlify.app, clickability affordance); fading edges as the scroll cue
(medium.com/headstorm, "In search of a better scroll UX pattern"; ux.stackexchange.com 90524).
The user confirmed the knob hover in the app ("it works").

## D205. The Pitch effect: Heads and Spectral, with a delay (2026-11-15)

The research on the user's reference artists (docs/research/sound-references.md) put a pitch effect first,
and the user chose to build it first. Asked how it should shift (options: both, as a Mode, recommended
and chosen; Heads only, as the research specified; Spectral only) and whether it gets a Delay so
Feedback steps each repeat (options: add Delay, recommended and chosen; no, as researched). A
fourteenth effect type, Pitch, for any layer or master slot (PitchInsert.h; DESIGN_V3.md section 8):
Pitch -24 to +24 semitones, Fine, Spread (left down, right up), Mix; Delay 0 to 1 s or a synced
Division, Feedback; Mode (Heads or Spectral), Window, Character (clean to rough).
- **Heads** is the Eventide H910/H949 delay-line shifter, de-glitched as the H949 was: the new head
  starts where its recent sound best matches the old head's (normalised correlation), and the
  crossfade's gains follow that correlation, so the level holds. Rough drops the search and
  shortens the crossfades: the H910 glitch. A shift up is low-passed first so nothing folds back.
- **Spectral** is the Laroche-Dolson peak-shifting phase vocoder (75 % overlap, fractional bin
  moves, phases turned per peak and so locked within its region); a new partial keeps its phase.
  Window picks its frame (21, 43 or 85 ms at 48 kHz), which is also how late it is. Rough rounds
  the moves to whole bins and, at the top, scatters phases: the phasey wash Loukeman likes.
- Measured (tests/V3PitchTests.cpp): intervals to 0.002 %, level within 1.5 dB; clean splices swing
  0.1 dB against 3.1 dB rough; no shift is transparent to -140 dB; mode and frame changes no rougher
  than steady playing. About 0.9 % of a core per slot in Heads and 2.9 % in Spectral.
- A shift in semitones (this and every other semitone value of a slot) now steps by whole semitones
  on a knob or the wheel, a tenth with Shift; a MiniLab knob takes six counts per semitone, as a list.
Sources: Eventide on the H949's de-glitching ("Ding Dong! The Glitch is Dead", eventideaudio.com);
Valhalla DSP, "Pitch Shifting: the H949, and de-glitching" (2010); Haghparast, Penttinen and
Valimaki, "Real-time pitch-shifting of musical signals by a time-varying factor using NFC-TSM",
DAFx-07; Laroche and Dolson, "New phase-vocoder techniques for pitch-shifting, harmonizing and other
exotic effects", IEEE WASPAA 1999 (ee.columbia.edu/~dpwe/papers/LaroD99-pvoc.pdf); AUTimePitch's
Tightness and Smoothness (lists.apple.com coreaudio-api, May 2005).

## D206. Night Bus, a room for the laptop-collage sound (2026-11-15)

The research proposed one new room for the space the user's references share; the user chose one
room. Asked its name (options: Night Bus, recommended and chosen; Afterparty; Box Room, closest to
the Bedroom) and its size (options: eight across the map, recommended; all sixteen, chosen; six,
as most rooms). resources/presets/13-night-bus.csbank, SOUND_BANK_V3.md section 3: Washed Poly, Tine
Clav, Buzz Drop, Square Grit, Whisper Air, Fizz Melody, Pirate Radio, Stutter Chords, Vocal Mask,
High Voice, Four-Track House, Spiral FM, Trance Glass, Bowed Haze, Stepped Lead, Sea Pad. Room rule:
the Pitch effect is in every preset, which keeps it apart from the Bedroom (tape) and the Basement
(drive). Two research proposals needed the user's own voice file; both play from factory sound
instead, so no preset is silent: Vocal Mask cuts the vocal wavetable into live grains (its comment
says how to put a file there), High Voice plays the factory choir. Spiral FM got the arpeggiator,
so one held key retriggers the pluck each sixteenth and the seven-step pattern is heard. Levels
from the loudness tool (D153), kept in each layer's level with master 0 for one-layer sounds (D194);
the three combinations (Tine Clav, Fizz Melody, Sea Pad) take it as master. Checked by
measurement before the user's ear: the patterns land (Spiral FM's seven notes, the stutter's gates,
Sea Pad's eighth-note bass, Stepped Lead's semitone trill), and no clip clicks except Pirate
Radio's deliberate crackle. The user's verdicts are on the audition page.

## D207. Slices and kits in the Sample generator (2026-11-15)

Second of the builds the research on the user's reference artists asked for (sample slices; the
user's order after the Pitch effect, D205). Asked, after looking at how Ableton's Simpler and Logic's
Quick Sampler slice: how to cut (options: equal parts and attacks, recommended and chosen; equal
parts only; equal, attacks and beats), a kit of several files (options: a folder is a kit,
recommended and chosen; later), how to choose a file (options: in the Sample list, recommended and
chosen; a file dialog button; both) and how a slice plays (options: to its end, overlapping,
recommended and chosen; to its end, one at a time; only while held).
- **The Sample slot's fourth page**: Slices (Off, Equal, Attacks), Count (2 to 64 equal pieces),
  Sensitivity (which attacks cut: (1 - sensitivity) squared of the strongest and up), First Key (C3,
  the MiniLab's lowest key, by default). One piece per key from First Key up; a key outside them is
  silent. A slice plays at the file's own speed on every key; the slot's octave and fine tuning,
  bend, glide and pitch modulation still move it. Reverse plays it from its end, Start skips into it;
  it fades in over 1 ms and out over its last 4 ms, so no cut clicks.
- **Attacks** are found once per file when it loads (UserLibrary::findAttacks): a spectral flux on
  log magnitudes over 5 ms hops, its peaks above their local median, 40 ms apart, each placed by the
  steepest rise of the sound's energy over 2 ms; a cut lies 2 ms before its attack, at least 20 ms
  after the last. Measured: five struck tones found within 1.5 ms. The file's own abrupt end, which
  also reads as an attack, is left out.
- **A folder is a kit**: its audio files in the order Windows Explorer shows them (numbers by their
  value), one per key from First Key, up to 64.
- **One-shot**: a slice or a kit's file plays to its end after the key is let go; the voice waits
  for it before releasing (holdsRelease). Slices overlap; with the layer in Mono a new key cuts the
  playing slice and plays its own (a choke, as Ableton's Mono does). Every other sound releases as
  before, sample for sample.
- **Choosing**: turning the Sample knob past the 40 instruments goes on into Your files: every
  file and every folder of audio inside the User folder of the sample library (made if missing),
  chosen with a press as an instrument is (D156); an instrument again lets the file go. With none,
  the list says where to put them.
- Saved states and sounds from before (processor state 13, patch 12) get the new values' defaults
  (they were saved as 0: C-1, two slices); Slices stays Off, so they sound the same.
Sources: Ableton Simpler's slicing (Sound On Sound, "Keep It Simple"; MusicRadar, "How to chop
beats with Ableton Simpler's Slice mode"); Logic Pro Quick Sampler's Slice mode and Sampler's
chromatic mapping of several files from C2 (support.apple.com, Logic Pro and MainStage guides).

## D208. A release to share: Release builds carry the runtime, the instruments go to Hugging Face (2026-11-18)

The user tested Night Bus and the Pitch effect and wants both the app and the VST3 released, first to
a friend (who will use the VST3), then on the website, with the README brought up to date; the user
tests the VST3 in Ableton first. Checked: the builds were Debug, which need Microsoft's debug runtime
DLLs (MSVCP140D, VCRUNTIME140D, ucrtbased), present only with Visual Studio and not to be passed on
(Microsoft Learn, "Prepare a test machine to run a debug executable"; "CRT library features").
- **Release builds link the C++ runtime statically (/MT)** (CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY); Debug keeps
  the DLLs as before. Checked with dumpbin: the Release app and VST3 need only Windows' own DLLs.
- **The instrument library (2.6 GB, CC0)** is hosted as a Hugging Face dataset, the user's choice
  (options: GitHub Releases, recommended; a Hugging Face dataset, chosen; Cloudflare R2 on an own
  address; an itch.io page). tools/release/package_library.py packs one zip per family (each with
  library.index; Keys, the largest, 941 MB) and writes the dataset card and checksums. The upload
  needs the user's own account. Sources: Hugging Face "Storage limits" (free public storage, best
  effort); GitHub "About releases" (2 GiB per file); Cloudflare R2 pricing; itch.io upload limits.
- **The README** describes CoolSynth as it is now (V3: two layers, the engine, the rooms, installing
  the VST3, the app and the library, one's own sounds, the MiniLab), with a current screenshot.
- **To test slices**, tools/audition/make_demo_user_sounds.py puts a demo kit (eight hits rendered
  from factory presets) and a two-bar demo loop in the User folder.
- Open before a public release: JUCE's licence (AGPLv3 or the JUCE licence's free Starter tier, the
  user's choice; forum.juce.com, the JUCE 8 EULA), and the version number.
- **The first Release test run crashed** (intermittently, after 38 groups) in "An instrument read in
  the background while an earlier sound rings does not click". Found with a crash handler that prints
  a symbolised stack (the test runner) and Release symbols in a separate .pdb (/Zi, /DEBUG, /OPT:REF,
  /OPT:ICF; the shipped binaries are unchanged): the test emptied the instrument library
  (SampleLibrary::setRoot) while its first render's held chord still counted itself a user of a
  style; destroying that render later wrote into the freed style. Debug's heap tolerated it. A test
  bug: the app and the plugin never empty the library while playing (setRoot is for tests and tools),
  now asserted in dropAll. The test finishes with each render before emptying the library.

## D209. A Windows installer that downloads the instruments; an icon (2026-11-18, 2026-11-19)

For the public release (the friend gets the public one, the user's choice). The user's choices, each from a pick list:
**JUCE's Starter licence** (recommended; CoolSynth is free and earns nothing), **version 0.3.0** (recommended),
**the instruments on Hugging Face** (a public dataset, `tomccargo/coolsynth-instruments`, the user signed in and I
uploaded; D208), **a dry run of the GitHub pipeline first** (recommended), **an Inno Setup installer that downloads
the instrument families** (recommended; the user asked for an installer that helps), **unsigned** (signing costs
money; Windows warns once), and the **icon**: option 1 of four variations on D, the user's own sketch: D's keyboard
of four white and three black keys unchanged, its green line gone, two teal knobs above it (options: two knobs,
chosen; two with one white; three; four in a row or two rows).
- **`installer/CoolSynth.iss`** (Inno Setup 6.5 or newer, for `.zip` extraction and downloads): the VST3 to
  `Common Files\VST3`, the app with a Start menu entry, and per instrument family a component that downloads its
  zip from the Hugging Face dataset, checks its SHA-256 and unpacks it into `C:\ProgramData\CoolSynth\Library`
  (`installer/Instruments.iss`, generated by `tools/release/make_installer_instruments.py` from the zips:
  sizes and hashes). Uninstalling removes the families and leaves the player's `User` folder. Types: everything;
  the VST3 and the app; choose. Since D211 it also adds the MiniLab 3 scripts for FL Studio and Ableton Live
  (`resources/daw-scripts`) to the player's Documents.
- **The icon**: `resources/icon/coolsynth-icon.svg`; `tools/release/make_icon.py` draws it into
  `installer/CoolSynth.ico` (16 to 256 px) and a 512 px PNG. The app and the VST3 keep JUCE's icon for now.
- **The pipeline**: the runner images do not promise Inno Setup (a community action notes its removal from
  future images), so both workflows install it from Chocolatey, pinned at 6.7.1 (the package's latest), and
  `scripts/ci/PackageRelease.ps1 -Installer` compiles `CoolSynth-Setup-<tag>.exe` beside the zips and in the
  checksum file; the release publishes it first.

## D211. Any controller plays the panel; the MiniLab driver in the VST3 too (2026-11-18)

A friend gets the release and will use the VST3 with a MIDI controller nobody knows. The user asked what
others do and chose (pick lists, with research): **all of it, plus ready setups for popular controllers**;
**the full MiniLab driver inside the VST3 as well**; **controllers first, then the release**.
- **The model.** A controller's control stands for one of the panel's *places* (the main knob, its press, one
  step left and right, Back, Shift, knobs 1-8 with knob 5 the pages, faders 1-4, pads 1-8) or for one sound
  *setting* on every page. A place follows the page, as the MiniLab's knobs do, so a controller works the
  panel as the MiniLab does. `src/midi/ControllerMap.h` (the map, JSON, the ready setups),
  `src/midi/ControllerSetup.h` (the computer's setup, learning, the audio thread's two questions),
  `src/interface/ControllerInput.h` (one CoolSynth's controller, played on its model). The V2 path
  (`MidiMappingEngine`, profiles mapping CCs to parameters, learned bindings in the processor's state) no longer
  runs in the processor; its classes stay for the standalone settings store until V2 goes.
- **Knobs with end stops: value scaling**, the user's choice (options: pick-up, recommended; value scaling,
  chosen; turns, like the MiniLab; jump). Ableton's Live 12 manual describes the same three takeover modes. The
  value moves the way the knob does, by the share of the room each has left, so the two meet at the ends and
  never jump; a list, a switch, an instrument or the pages move an option when the scaled place passes the next
  one (`InterfaceModel::moveKnobFromController`). Endless knobs turn as the MiniLab's do (D200); faders are
  taken as they stand (D184). A control a ready setup calls a position that sends the same value twice, away
  from its ends, is taken as turning from then on: a ready setup may not know how the keyboard was set.
- **Learn: both, chosen in the menu**, the user's choice (options: both, recommended and chosen; the place
  only; the setting only). A right click on a knob, fader or pad offers "Learn for knob 3 (follows the page)"
  and, on knobs 1-4 and 6-8, "Learn for Cutoff (on every page)", and Forget for either.
- **Kept on this computer**, the user's choice (options: this computer for everything, recommended and chosen;
  the setup on the computer and setting learns in the project; everything in the project):
  `%APPDATA%\CoolSynth\controller.json`, shared by the app and every plugin, re-read when another program saves
  it. A state's own learned controls (before D211) are no longer read or written.
- **A known controller is used at once, and the screen says so**, the user's choice (options: use it and say so,
  recommended and chosen; ask first; only from the setup). With nothing saved, CoolSynth looks at the MIDI
  inputs every few seconds; a plugin cannot tell which input feeds its track, so with two known controllers the
  first wins.
- **Ready setups** (from the makers' manuals and setup guides, checked 2026-11-18): Akai MPK mini mk3 (program 1:
  knobs CC 70-77, pads notes 36-43 on channel 10), Novation Launchkey Mini MK3 and MK4 (knobs CC 21-28 as they
  start), Arturia KeyLab Essential mk3 (User 1: knobs 74 71 76 77 93 18 19 16, faders 73 75 79 72, pads 36-43),
  Arturia MiniLab 3 in its Arturia program (the V2 profile's CCs, tested on the device then; main knob CC 114),
  NI Komplete Kontrol M32 and A series in MIDI mode (knobs CC 14-21). Where a maker states no channel, the setup
  takes any. Several of these numbers come from a third party's setup guides (Spectrasonics) rather than the
  maker; the MPK Mini IV, MPK mini Plus and Oxygen Pro Mini state no factory numbers and have none.
- **The guided setup** (Preferences > Controller setup): asks for each place in turn and listens; a turn is
  told from a position by repeats (a position never repeats a value away from its ends), and its encoding (64
  +/- steps, two's complement, sign bit) from the values heard turning right, then left. A button heard for the
  main knob makes it one step left, and the next step asks for right.
- **What is linked is not the sound's**: a linked control's messages go to the panel and not to the sound (a
  pad plays no note); a note's release always reaches the sound. Mod wheel, sustain, bank select, data entry and
  the channel-mode messages can never be linked.
- **The MiniLab driver in the VST3.** Windows MIDI Services (in-box since February 2026, Windows 11 24H2 and
  later) routes the old WinMM calls through a service, so two programs can open the same port; checked on this
  computer: two programs opened "Minilab3 MIDI" in and out at once. The driver moved to `src/interface/`; in a
  plugin the host brings the keys and every CoolSynth there drops the MiniLab's control messages the host passes
  on. **Who drives it**, the user's choice (options: the window opened last, recommended and chosen; the first
  one loaded keeps it; a "MiniLab here" button): opening a CoolSynth's window takes it; until then the first
  CoolSynth loaded has it; between programs (the app, a DAW) the first to take it keeps it until it closes (a
  named lock). The driver starts a second after the plugin is made, so a host's plugin scan never wakes the
  MiniLab. The DAW's own MiniLab control surface must be set to None, or both talk to it; the screen says so.
  Without Windows MIDI Services the port cannot be shared and the driver keeps trying.
- **A switch for the MiniLab driver**, the user's choice, so the user can try what the friend will see (options: a
  driver switch, recommended and chosen; test with keys only; test the MiniLab driver only). Preferences >
  Controller setup > MiniLab 3 driver On / Off, kept in controller.json. Off, the driver lets the MiniLab go back to
  its own program (in the app its port becomes one of the app's MIDI inputs again), and its ready setup applies.
- **Found in the user's Ableton test** (2026-11-19): Ableton gives an instrument every note on channel 1 (Ableton
  forum, several threads), so the MiniLab's pads (channel 10) played notes: the driver now marks each pad it hears
  on the MiniLab's own port, and the host's copy of that note, arriving a moment later, is kept from the sound
  (`MiniLab3Driver::isPadEcho`). And the arpeggiator was silent while a DAW knew its tempo but was not playing:
  now a key starts it at the DAW's tempo; while the DAW plays it locks to the bar as before, and the DAW's stop
  stops it until the next key.
- **Pads in FL Studio: DAW scripts**, the user's choice after research (2026-11-19). In FL the pads still played
  notes: FL runs a plugin in pieces of a few samples, so the host's copy of a pad's note reaches the sound before the
  driver hears the pad on the MiniLab's own port, and `isPadEcho` (a timing guess) misses it. How others do it:
  controllers put their DAW-mode controls on a second port the tracks do not listen to (Novation's Launchkey, "MIDI
  IN2"), or a DAW script takes them (Ableton: messages a control surface uses "can no longer be used as input for
  MIDI tracks"; Arturia's and Novation's own integrations); or the track listens to the keys' channel only. The
  MiniLab 3 sends pads and keys on the same port, so the second-port way is out. Options: DAW scripts that claim
  the pads, recommended and chosen; the track set to channel 1 by the user; holding pad notes back up to 5 ms
  (rejected: the user wants no delay). `resources/daw-scripts/`: an FL Studio script (`device_CoolSynth_MiniLab.py`,
  `OnMidiIn` marks channel-10 notes 36-51 handled) and an Ableton control surface (`CoolSynth_MiniLab`, a
  `_Framework` script that listens to those notes and sends nothing). FL links the script by itself through its
  `supportedDevices` header (FL Studio Python API, script metadata); where FL had already linked its own Arturia
  MiniLab 3 script (on this computer), the link was switched in FL's registry (`Devices\MIDI input\Minilab3 MIDI`,
  `ScriptFolder`). The Ableton script is chosen once as a control surface: Ableton loads scripts by itself only by
  USB ID (`get_capabilities`), and the MiniLab's port order for that is not known for sure, so it is left out.
  `isPadEcho` stays as a fallback where no script is chosen.
- **FL Studio's slivers** (2026-11-19): FL runs a plugin in pieces of a few samples at each event; the flight
  recorder called each a dropout (943 in ten minutes) though the audio thread was busy 1.4 to 4.8% of each second
  and the worst block took 0.55 ms of 3 to 5. Blocks under 64 samples no longer raise the alarm; their time still
  counts in the second's summary.
- **The review of D211** (2026-11-19): a review of the day's code; the user chose to fix everything it found.
  - *The controller queue*: the audio thread finishes writing an event before it tells the message thread (told
    first, the event could wait for the next one); events left after eight rounds go at the next tick.
  - *Learning belongs to the CoolSynth that asked*: `learn` takes the asking CoolSynth's controller input and
    returns a token. A host feeding two CoolSynths one keyboard brings every move twice; only the owner's count. An
    older window closing no longer ends a newer learning; a card whose learning was taken over waits, then listens
    again. Learning for a button turns down a knob or fader and says so.
  - *Endless knobs*: a control a ready setup calls a position is taken as endless only after three equal values
    such a knob sends for one step (61-67 but 64, 1-3, 125-126); other repeats (a resend, a 14-bit fader's coarse
    half) are no evidence, so a knob is never taken for an endless one by chance. A changed setup forgets what was
    guessed. Values 0 or 97 and up only are two's complement turned left.
  - *FL Studio*: the load that decides when sounds are cut is measured over at least 64 samples, so FL's slivers no
    longer look like overload and cut tails. The FL script takes the pads only while a CoolSynth is in the project
    (FL's `channels.channelCount` and `plugins.getPluginName`); without one they play notes in FL as usual.
  - *The MiniLab*: a host's note is a pad's echo only while that pad is held and when it came after the key, within
    250 ms, so a low key played after a pad is no longer lost. The user's sounds folder is read at most once a second
    while a controller's knob with end stops moves through it (each of its messages read it); a turn still reads it
    each time, so files just dropped in show at once. The setup's notices wait while Learn's prompt is on the screen.
  - *The installer*: an elevated installer's `{userdocs}` is the administrator's (Inno Setup's [Run] section help;
    its mailing list, "RunAsOriginalUser"), so the scripts go to the install folder and
    `installer/Install-MiniLabScripts.ps1` copies them on, run as the player who started the installer
    (`runasoriginaluser`); it finds Documents where Windows keeps it, OneDrive's included. Uninstalling removes them
    from the Documents of the account that approved it.
  - *Leaving*: the setup's timers go with the last CoolSynth in the program, while JUCE still runs (Debug warned at
    exit). Tests: learning's owner and token, a knob refused for a button, a repeated position that is no turn, the
    setup file and its switch kept and read back, a block full of controller moves and an overflow, and the DAW's
    stop stopping the arp until the next key.

## D212. A layer's right-click menu, Stop, the version in Preferences, Reese to start (2026-11-19)

The user asked for four things before the release.
- **A right click on a layer panel** (the screen's LAYER A and LAYER B; not on the MiniLab) offers the Layers page's
  actions (D196), worded for the layer clicked whichever one the sections edit: copy it to the other, copy the other
  to it, swap, double it onto the other, it an octave lower or higher on the other, solo it (ticked while on), empty
  the other. As the page has them, so the two never differ; HALion's functions menu has a Copy Layer command alike
  (Steinberg's HALion manual). A left click still picks the layer to edit.
- **Stop** (the fifth pad, on the screen and the MiniLab; a recorder pad until now) ends every sound, as MIDI's All
  Sound Off: at once whatever the pedal or the release, the arps, and the delay's and reverb's tails, which a panic
  is expected to clear (MIDI 1.0's wording of All Sound Off; Nord user forum on panic and effects). The engine's
  panic already cleared all of it; nothing called it. It cut the sound in the middle of a wave, which clicks: Stop
  now lets the block it lands in fade to silence over 3 ms, then clears everything. The keys of that block are dropped.
  The user's test found sound after Stop with Acid on both layers: the Drive effect's 2x filters and tone filter were
  not cleared by the rack's clear or reset, and played their last samples one block later (Acid and Reese use Drive;
  Basic Saw, Moog Bass and Supersaw did not show it). Both now reset them. A tone held on in the user's test was most
  likely the CoolSynth in Ableton, open beside the app, playing the same held MiniLab key.
- **The version in Preferences**, the user's choice of place (not beside the CPU reading): small and faint at the
  right of the title, "CoolSynth 0.3.0", with CoolSynth's icon left of the title. The version comes from
  CMakeLists.txt's project version through the generated build header (`coolsynth::build::getVersion`), so it is never
  typed in twice; the icon is `resources/icon/coolsynth-icon-512.png`, embedded and halved step by step to 64 px.
- **A new CoolSynth starts on Reese** (Main Stage), the user's choice, instead of Contact (D193); first Supersaw, then
  "change my mind: make Reese the default, same category". Reset the sound comes back to it, and Preferences' Reset
  line names it from the same constant.
